the denylistComplete comment/code disagreement could NOT be substantiated on current main (aggregator.ts:330-352 — comment and code agree, the tradeoff is self-documented) — drop that half — and a fresh re-measure plus fix landed 2026-09-24 (run 04c7d9c2 assess d2a81faa + implement 9b17c4e7, base 763c1fa): the cache-TTL no-op RESOLVED BY REMOVAL — CACHE_TTL_MS (60s) equaled the refresh interval with a strict less-than validity check (aggregator.ts:717-718 vs :792), so steady-state revisit ages sat at the interval plus-or-minus per-repo fetch-latency variance — hits possible only for timing-lucky entries, not meaningfully above zero, dead weight; negative caching for dead installations DECLINED — the 60s refresh cadence bounds re-hits to one resolve call per minute per dead repo and the skip path already warns each cycle, so growing the auth-path failure surface is not worth the marginal API saving (revisit alongside rm-141 concurrency if the budget ever binds)

### Single-source the SSE parser invariants
- id: `rm-114` | track: reliability | priority: 45.0 | status: candidate (from run 270220e7 assess F5/F6)
- signals: the server reader and the browser parser are maintained by hand in parallel and share a live bug — the buffer cap counts UTF-16 code units against a BYTES-named constant (`src/gateway/operator-sse-reader.ts:35,526` and `public/operator-stream.js:44,2473`); the first-frame timeout park (operator-stream.js:2505) mirrors the same duplication — 2026-09-25 update (run 4e7c674 roadmap): 2026-09-25 assess F10 sharpened the shared-divergence list: parseSseRecord keeps only the last `data:` line of a multi-line record where SSE spec joins with \n (a gateway that ever multi-lines frames fails closed as a JSON error), and MAX_SSE_BUFFER_BYTES counts UTF-16 code units, not bytes — astral-plane text can ~3x the real byte budget before tripping (both persist at 480c92c); second shared bug (2026-09-24, run 41c7d471 assess F7, first reported by sibling run d6455cdc and never tracked): both parsers keep only the LAST `data:` line of an SSE frame (src/gateway/operator-sse-reader.ts:116-117, public/operator-stream.js:205+) — legal multi-`data:` frames are silently truncated to their final fragment (the gateway emits single-line frames today, so the parser contract is what's wrong, not live traffic) (2026-09-25, cycle-13 batch B2 implement 04463ee8: the UNITS half is fixed on both halves — the cap now enforces UTF-8 bytes via incremental TextEncoder counters in the server reader and the browser twin, with astral-input tests pinning the corrected semantics ('𝕏' chunks over the byte cap but under the code-unit count must fail closed); the shared-source extraction and the multi-`data:` newline-join remain open in this item; landed at the aaa84dff integrate into 828a1e6)
- acceptance: cap constant and normalization rules extracted to one shared source consumed by both the server reader and the browser bundle (shared module or build-time generation); divergence becomes a build/type failure rather than a live parser bug; multi-byte frame tests cover the corrected cap semantics; the shared source also joins multi-`data:` frame lines per SSE semantics (newline join) so both consumers agree on legal multi-data frames (added 2026-09-24, run 41c7d471 assess F7)
- evidence: single definition site; `pnpm test` includes multi-byte boundary-frame tests; grep shows no remaining duplicated literal; property-suite cases cover multi-data frames

### Optional trusted-proxy mode for the ingest rate limiter
- id: `rm-129` | track: security | priority: 40.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-24 (cycle-10 B1 + review fix: per-path-class budgets — public/operator/ingest, each with its own `RATE_LIMIT_MAX_*` env, ingest is its OWN budget not an exemption — plus opt-in `rateLimitTrustedProxy`/`RATE_LIMIT_TRUSTED_PROXY` XFF first-hop re-key (safe only behind an XFF-overwriting proxy; key capped at 64 chars), legacy bare-call 429-compat preserved; 68/68 focused + 5-test env config-surface suite (override honored, invalid falls back to default per the house env convention — the acceptance's fail-fast wording deliberately not adopted — proxy flag only on {1,true,yes}, cap collapses overlong tokens) + AGENTS.md env note; LANDED — completed at origin/main 539c632 via the cycle-10 lineage, verified live by run 5b6e8e801952 assess F2 (server.ts:577-611 per-path-class budgets + opt-in trusted-proxy re-key in-tree); follow-on default-topology signal folded into rm-178)
- signals: the listener-ingest rate limiter keys on the direct connection address and deliberately ignores `X-Forwarded-For` as client-spoofable (src/server.ts:520-535 comment) — correct standalone, but behind the gateway reverse-proxy topology (rm-127's assumed deployment) every producer shares ONE bucket and a single noisy producer throttles all others; no configured middle ground exists today (fresh currency 2026-09-24, run 3538ec96 assess F1: limiter unchanged at 82312f7 (server.ts:79-81 config, :520-530 direct-address keying, :553-582 isPublicPath incl. /api/healthz); research idea 2 argues the middle ground should pair the XFF re-key with path-class budgets — public healthz/auth, operator, ingest — so a flooder on the public class cannot consume the operator's budget even in standalone mode); prior art 2026-09-24 (run 91e4626d research): the upstream gateway solved this exact class at fro-bot/agent v0.114.1 (PR #1651) — operator sign-in/rate limits key on the resolved client behind a reverse proxy, forwarding metadata accepted only from configured trusted proxies, and missing/malformed/ambiguous forwarding is REJECTED rather than guessed; main already pins v0.114.1 (fro-bot.yaml:337), so deployments enabling the proxied operator surface must set GATEWAY_OPERATOR_TRUSTED_PROXIES now — the docs/runbooks/gateway-access.md note rides the implementation; the unset-stays-spoof-safe shape matches this item's acceptance verbatim; the cycle-8 batch (pending PR b9c36244) pairs the gateway-side deployment with its own runbook/config item (its rm-150) — keep the two synchronized
- acceptance: an explicit trusted-proxy setting — unset keeps today's behavior (XFF ignored, spoof-safe); set switches rate-limit keying to the validated X-Forwarded-For first hop; both modes tested including the spoof-rejection case when unset, and the trade-off is documented next to the existing comment
- evidence: rate-limiter tests in `pnpm test` for both modes; a config-surface test including fail-fast on invalid values; an AGENTS.md or config-doc note naming the proxy assumption

### CSRF tokens for listener ack mutations
- id: `rm-115` | track: security | priority: 30.0 | status: completed (implemented 2026-09-24 as B4 of docs/prioritization/2026-09-24-cycle-1-batch.md, run 6cbc2a1d implement attempt a292009c; landed 2026-09-26 at this integrate of run 6cbc2a1d into main's 0ca643e lineage — conflict case f3c5735b; B4 rides unchanged from the batch's implement tree, no re-implementation needed)
- signals: `src/routes/listener.ts:121,130` accept session-authenticated state-changing POSTs (`/messages/:id/ack`, `/ack-all`) protected only by SameSite=Lax cookies, while logout already implements an explicit CSRF-token dance (`src/routes/auth.ts:168-190`) — an asymmetry; exploit window is narrow (Lax blocks cross-site POSTs except the fresh-cookie grace period) and impact bounded (ack state)
- acceptance: ack mutations require the same csrf-token pattern as logout (or equivalent origin check), with tests for the rejected cross-origin case; no behavior change for the operator's own client
- evidence: route tests in `pnpm test` covering token-missing rejection; manual operator flow unchanged
- implementation (2026-09-24; landed at the 2026-09-26 integrate of run 6cbc2a1d): `deriveAckCsrfToken` (HMAC-SHA256 over the cookie key + operator login, 1h validity window with current+previous acceptance) mirrors the logout dance; `GET /api/listener/csrf` mints the token; both mutations verify via `x-csrf-token` with `timingSafeEqual` and fail closed 403 on missing/invalid token or unavailable CSRF material (503 when standalone-config lacks the cookie key); web client fetches the token per mutation and never posts without one; contract updated in `docs/contracts/operator-listener-channel.md`; 5 new server route tests (incl. wrong-operator 403 and standalone-null-config paths) + 5 ack-flow web client tests (3 updated, 2 net-new fail-closed), all in the 3127-test green run

### Cookie-key canonical-format enforcement
- id: `rm-206` | track: security | priority: 55.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-25 (landed at the c2ae28ab integrate — authored in-batch as rm-165, renumbered because landed meanings own ids; conflict case 6a7122e8)
- signals: `loadCookieKey`/`decodeKey` previously accepted lenient decodings — stripped-padding base64, non-canonical hex/base64 lengths, and raw UTF-8 passphrases all silently loaded via Node's lenient decoders, so a misprovisioned cookie key booted and signed instead of failing at startup; a 44-char all-hex input fell through to a base64 mis-decode (the "44 A's" exploit path yielding 33 null bytes)
- acceptance: the encoded key form must be the exact canonical encoding of its decoded bytes — hex at 2 chars/byte or padded base64 at 4 chars per 3 bytes, decoding to ≥32 bytes; non-canonical encodings, degenerate (single-repeated-byte) keys, and raw passphrases throw fail-fast with specific messages at load; documented in README.md's Configuration section
- evidence: src/session.ts `decodeKey`/`canonicalFormatError`/`assertNonDegenerate`; test/session.test.ts `canonical format enforcement (rm-206)` suite (raw passphrase rejected, stripped padding rejected, 44-A hex-then-base64 fallthrough rejected, canonical forms accepted)

### Unread badge silently freezes on auth expiry or network failure
- id: `rm-208` | track: operator-experience | priority: 32.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-29 (folded into cycle-19 B2 with rm-268, run 5e7cb89f implement 075c2ae5 — the App badge poll stops on reason 'unauthenticated' (interval+focus loop halted via the authExpired guard, platform badge cleared, unread-auth-expired affordance handed to the shell via listenerUnreadAuthExpired) and marks the rendered count stale with title+aria carrying the last-good epoch after the two-failure streak (App.tsx rm-208 markers; App.test.tsx pins poll teardown + the stale streak); carried twice by surface-collision discipline since 2026-09-25; full_tests 206a418e 10/10 green; pending landing; original: added 2026-09-25, run 71991cb3 assess F3 + research A2)
- signals: `web/src/App.tsx:45-56` pollUnreadCount calls fetchListenerMessages every 30s but only acts on success (`if (res.ok) setUnreadCount(...)`, no else branch) — on 401 or network failure the count freezes at its last good value with the interval continuing forever; the operator runtime it feeds (public/operator-*) DOES surface auth-required states, so the SPA is the only surface that hides expiry; no stale/auth indicator exists anywhere in the badge path; compound 2026-09-25 (run 71991cb3 compound d730c0da): deliberately NOT in the cycle-17 batch (surface-collision discipline — this run owned workflow/census surfaces only) and carried as the LEADING operator-experience candidate for the next cycle: auth-expired badge state + stale indicator with last-good timestamp + poll-loop test mocks, per this item's acceptance
- acceptance: a 401 stops the poll loop and renders an auth-expired state (badge cleared, re-auth affordance consistent with existing session UX); a network-failure streak of ≥2 marks the badge stale (aria + title with last-good timestamp) instead of freezing silently; focused tests in web/src/App.test.tsx cover ok/401/network-resolved mocks for the poll loop
- evidence: new App.test.tsx cases green (`pnpm test`); manual 30s-expiry check noted in the PR body or a docs/solutions entry

- id: `rm-155` | track: reliability | priority: 42.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-24 (cycle-9 batch U2, run 91e4626d — targeted 696/696 + full ephemeral-CI PR #38 ok; review/landing pending)
- signals: `web/src/views/Listener.tsx:21-33` — isFetchingRef is set before the await and released only after it settles, and the AbortController created at :31 is never aborted (no unmount cleanup, no timeout), so a single hung `/api/listener/messages` fetch permanently disables the 30s poll while the error UI's retry promise is false in that state; `web/src/App.tsx:45-56` — pollUnreadCount has no in-flight guard, swallows failures silently (`if (res.ok)` with no else — the badge freezes stale on gateway outage or auth expiry with no signal), and the 30s interval keeps firing in hidden tabs
- 2026-09-29 signal update (run 262f170c implement, rm-258's prune): the signals above are HISTORICAL — they describe the pre-fix code the 2026-09-24 U2 landing replaced; landed Listener.tsx releases the latch in a finally, races a 15s timeout, and aborts on unmount (tests at web/src/views/Listener.test.tsx rm-155 block), and the App.tsx half is owned by rm-209/215. As of this date the pattern is further extracted into web/src/hooks/useBoundedPoll.ts and consumed by BOTH polling views (rm-251); the only live remainder of this item's acceptance is the api/listener.ts self-bound contract-sync half
- acceptance: the Listener poll releases its latch in a finally block and aborts in-flight fetches on unmount (controller wired to the request signal), with a regression test seeding a never-settling fetch and asserting the next tick still polls; the App poll gains an in-flight guard, surfaces poll failure once (silent recovery allowed), and pauses while document.hidden (visibilitychange resumes with an immediate poll); no behavior change on the happy path
- evidence: pnpm test additions covering the hung-fetch latch release and the hidden-tab pause; manual verification that a killed dev server leaves a visible error state instead of a silently frozen badge

### Outbound HTTP timeouts and refresh watchdog
- id: `rm-156` | track: reliability | priority: 60.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-24 (cycle-9 batch U1, run 91e4626d — targeted 696/696 + full ephemeral-CI PR #38 ok; review/landing pending)
- signals: zero timeout configuration on any outbound server HTTP — grep for AbortSignal/timeout/signal across src/ returns only comments; src/server.ts:963 constructs graphql.defaults({...}) without a timeout option, the Octokit clients in src/github/app-client.ts ride defaults, and the gateway OperatorClient fetches (src/gateway/operator-server-fetch.ts) carry no signal — undici's default ~300s headers/body timeout is the only ceiling, so each hung upstream can stall the aggregator's serial per-repo walk (rm-141) for minutes while the in-flight guard skips ticks and staleBanner stays false (rm-112 lineage); no consumer can notice today (rm-126) (implementation finding 2026-09-24, run 91e4626d cycle-9 batch: the `timeout` Octokit option is INERT against a hung upstream on this runtime and graphql never forwards per-request signals — the only honored seam is `request: {fetch}` injection, so the shipped bound is createBoundedFetch(15s) via AbortSignal.any(callerSignal, AbortSignal.timeout) on the constructor, the auth-app mint, and graphql.defaults; ALSO @octokit/plugin-retry retried the timeout abort (surfacing as 500) 3× with backoff, multiplying the stall — retries disabled for this client; full lesson in docs/solutions/runtime-errors/octokit-timeout-option-inert-on-hung-upstreams-bind-at-fetch-layer-2026-09-24.md; true remaining unbounded-surface delta vs this item's original claim was the GitHub data plane only — operator-server-fetch.ts:139 (10s) and oauth.ts:78/132 were already bounded, per the stewardship re-verification)
- acceptance: every outbound client carries an explicit timeout sized per call class (metadata read, GraphQL, gateway proxy, token mint) — the @octokit request timeout option and AbortSignal.timeout() on raw fetches are the standard primitives; the aggregator records refresh duration and a watchdog marks the snapshot degraded when a refresh exceeds a documented ceiling, feeding rm-107's composed status surface; tests inject a hung upstream and assert bounded cycle time plus the degradation marker
- evidence: pnpm test with a socket-stall fixture proving the timeout fires; aggregator diff showing duration capture; repo-wide grep shows no unconfigured outbound client remains

### OAuth PKCE (S256) for the operator login flow
- id: `rm-149` | track: security | priority: 66.0 | status: candidate (added 2026-09-23, run b9c36244 research C1 + assess; still open at the 2026-09-26 cycle-8 integrate — grep code_challenge across src/ and web/src/ returns zero on main f5686a6; the cycle-9 auth-theme headliner handoff never picked it up)
- signals: the OAuth authorization redirect sends state-only CSRF protection with no code_challenge (src/routes/auth.ts; grep code_challenge across src/ and web/src/ returns zero at 7809df6), while GitHub's own OAuth docs now document PKCE for OAuth Apps as strongly recommended — code_challenge + code_challenge_method S256 (plain unsupported), with the verifier storable in a cookie alongside state (github/docs content/apps/oauth-apps/building-oauth-apps/authorizing-oauth-apps.md lines 86-113, fetched 2026-09-23) (2026-09-24 compound, run b9c36244 prioritize+compound: DEFERRED past cycle 8 by selection despite the strongest research evidence — an Effort-2 auth surface violates the single-cycle pattern; nominated the cycle-9 headliner as a paired auth theme with rm-104 + rm-127 per docs/prioritization/2026-09-23-cycle-8-batch.md deferrals) (2026-09-30 github-review, run c06f7bf3d796 review of PR #237: CodeQL flagged the UNLANDED PKCE candidate on ephemeral validation snapshots — code-scanning alerts #74 (js/insufficient-password-hash, src/routes/auth.ts:37, instance refs/pull/230/merge) + #75 (same rule, test/auth-pkce.test.ts:125, instance refs/pull/237/merge), both false-positive-in-substance since RFC 7636 mandates S256 = BASE64URL(SHA-256(verifier)) and the flagged cookie-sourced value is a PKCE code_verifier, not a stored password; both alerts state-null on deleted refs and zero flagged code exists on main (code_challenge grep empty at 278d350); implement-time constraint so the future landing does not re-raise this on main: author the S256 sites alert-clean — first restructure the test binding assertion to hash a verifier captured BEFORE it enters a cookie (kills the getSetCookie-to-createHash taint source), else a scoped inline `'// codeql[js/insufficient-password-hash]'` with an RFC 7636 justification comment, else post-landing dismiss-with-reason (needs a security_events-scoped token; repo PAT scopes are repo/gist/read:org))
- acceptance: the authorization redirect carries state AND an S256 code_challenge; the verifier lives in a short-lived HttpOnly SameSite=Lax cookie mirroring the state pattern; the token exchange sends code_verifier and rejects mismatches; tests cover challenge-absent rejection at exchange, verifier-mismatch rejection, and the happy path; grep shows code_challenge wired at the single redirect builder
- evidence: new auth tests in pnpm test; grep code_challenge hits at src/routes/auth.ts; any fork-specific nuance recorded in docs/solutions/

### Restore client push delivery via a minimal push-only service worker
- id: `rm-249` | track: operator-experience | priority: 72.0 | status: candidate (added 2026-09-23 by run 998b4953's roadmap phase as rm-143 and DEFERRED by its prioritize step — 'not next-cycle material unless rm-106's user-facing dead-notification UI gets a product decision'; renumbered at the 2026-09-26 integrate, conflict case 9f604f39 — main's landed rm-143 (security-posture docs) owns that id; prerequisite for rm-106, decision joint with rm-138; dated rider 2026-09-29, run 5e7cb89f research C1: standards grounding for the substrate — WebKit ships Web Push on iOS/iPadOS 16.4+ only for Home Screen web apps (webkit.org/blog/13878), so the 'ios-not-installed' state is correct and the install path is load-bearing; but the repo's icons are SVG-only (web/index.html points apple-touch-icon at /icon-192.svg; web/public/manifest.webmanifest lists SVG icons only) while Apple's Safari Web Content Guide specifies web-clip icons in PNG format — Safari will not use the SVG, degrading the exact Add-to-Home-Screen step iOS push depends on, so the substrate carries a PNG-icon rider (192/512 PNG + PNG apple-touch-icon); and the push SW must own PushSubscription.expirationTime / pushsubscriptionchange rotation (W3C Push API) which the post-hoc reconcile sweep cannot observe — see rm-267 for the sweep's unbounded await)

- acceptance: a minimal push-only service worker registered alongside the existing shell (no fetch handler, no precache manifest — compatible with either rm-138 outcome), wired through sw-notification.ts's orphaned helpers; subscribe.ts reaches a real subscription or a distinguishable failure state; the joint decision with rm-138 recorded in the cycle batch doc; rm-106's digest/push delivery rides it once the gateway-side send contract (rm-106's first dependency discovery) exists
- evidence: web tests for the push/notificationclick handlers and the subscribe path; manual verification on an installed PWA receiving a live VAPID push; README's /sw.js claim truthed

- id: `rm-482` | track: reliability | priority: 30.0 | status: implemented 2026-10-03 (minted at this integrate — batch B2a of run 4084c786aa67, authored 2026-09-26 at base 7809df6 as that batch's rm-140 rider and landing with this merge; renumbered per the ledger convention: main's landed ledger owns rm-134..rm-140 with other meanings, all-lineage ceiling was rm-481)
- signals: web/src/push/subscribe.ts mintIdempotencyKey() previously fell back to Date.now+Math.random when crypto.randomUUID was unavailable — an idempotency key minted from randomness defeats the key's purpose (retries mint new keys, re-delivering the subscription) and the fallback masked the missing-crypto condition instead of failing the request

### Stranded SSE connections on drift and first-frame-timeout exits
- id: `rm-261` | track: reliability | priority: 56.0 | status: completed (landed 2026-09-29 at the integrate, conflict case 29354f50 — code and tests ride this merge into main; batch frame: 2026-09-29 cycle-18 implement b9103a41 at d89ffe7; targeted 360/360 + impacted 705/705 + full CI 10/10 green on ephemeral PR #254 — minted as rm-251 at base d89ffe7, renumbered above the all-lineage max rm-260 — main's rm-251 (run 262f170c bounded-poll cycle, landed at 38afd90) owns that id)
- signals: the operator SSE read loop exits on the 'drift' and 'submitted-unobservable' transitions with a bare return (public/operator-stream.js:2529-2535) leaving the body reader locked and the fetch un-aborted; the AbortController is aborted only on buffer-overflow (:2498-2500) and in close() (:2577-2578), and connect() REPLACES the controller without aborting the prior one (:2401); the first-frame-timeout doc comment says "A manual retry re-opens" — each retry after a 15s stalled run strands another open origin connection, and browsers cap ~6 HTTP/1.1 connections per origin, so repeated stalled runs starve ALL same-origin traffic (React polls, CSRF refresh); the gateway holds each stream session open against a stalled reader meanwhile
- acceptance: every non-close exit from the read loop either cancels the reader or aborts the controller (drift, submitted-unobservable, and the http-status paths audited in the same pass); connect() aborts any prior live controller before replacing it; a regression test drives first-frame-timeout then manual retry repeatedly and asserts the number of live connections stays at one (fetch/abort spy counts)
- evidence: three rm-261 regression tests in test/operator-stream-core.test.ts drive drift/trailing-record and 15s first-frame-timeout with a fetch stub recording signals — each asserts the controller's signal aborted; operator-stream-core 360/360, targeted 439/439 across touched suites, full CI green (PR #254); pattern doc: docs/solutions/workflow-issues/sse-read-loop-abort-lifecycle-2026-09-29.md

- id: `rm-272` | track: reliability | priority: 74.0 | status: completed 2026-09-29 (landed at the integrate via conflict case 33e48330 — minted as rm-267 at base 163ff11 and renumbered above the all-lineage max rm-271 (main's rm-267 — installations enumeration fetch seam, landed at 65f05db via conflict case 709f024e — owns that id); cycle-19 B1, run 5e7cb89f implement 075c2ae5; full_tests 206a418e ephemeral-CI 10/10 green on this exact tree; original: added 2026-09-29, run 5e7cb89f assess F1; concrete defect in the cycle-16..18 push product surface — pairs with rm-249's substrate decision; dated rider 2026-09-29, run 5e7cb89f implement B1: IMPLEMENTED — subscribe.ts runReconcileSweep + unsubscribeOptOut local reads bounded by withTimeout(DEFAULT_LOCAL_READ_TIMEOUT_MS) behind an injectable localReadTimeoutMs dep (the sweep's never-settling read now settles as no-local-subscription and derives conservatively to action 'none'), and subscribeOptIn's ready-timeout path probes getSwRegistration (defaults to navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistration, rejection collapsed to no-registration) to classify the permanent case as a NEW 'sw-unavailable' outcome distinct from transient 'sw-not-ready'; notifications-copy.ts gains the sw-unavailable copy (no registration — reinstall/reload needed, not initializing) and Notifications.tsx consumes it at the outcome + subscribe state + copy sites; pinned by subscribe.test.ts (bounded sweep settles + no destructive action + second per-event sweep also settles, sw-unavailable for absent + rejecting probe, transient sw-not-ready still reachable with a live registration) and notifications-copy.test.ts state list)
- signals: web/src/push/subscribe.ts:624 (runReconcileSweep) awaits `deps.getLocalSubscription()` with no bound, and every caller wires it to `navigator.serviceWorker.ready` (web/src/views/Notifications.tsx:100,151,163,309) — `serviceWorker.ready` never settles when no registration exists, and nothing in the shipped SPA creates one (grep for serviceWorker.register/registerSW across web/src, web/public, web/index.html: zero hits; web/src/main.tsx is 19 lines with no registration; web/src/sw.ts is the self-unregistering kill-switch). The same file wraps its sibling serviceWorkerReady calls in withTimeout (:325, :426, and the resubscribe window at :289-291) — the sweep path is the one unbounded await. Consequences in the flag-enabled configuration (DASHBOARD_PUSH_NOTIFICATIONS_ENABLED, the only configuration the feature exists for): the mount sweep and every debounced focus/visibility sweep accumulate never-settling promises, the reconcile cache state never advances past initial so the min-interval throttle never arms, and Enable terminates at 'sw-not-ready' whose copy (web/src/views/notifications-copy.ts:73-75) says the worker is "initializing or temporarily unavailable" — a permanent condition presented as transient. Cycle-18's rm-264 hardened the subscribe POST abort windows but not the sweep entry
- acceptance: the sweep's getLocalSubscription path is bounded by the same withTimeout discipline as :325/:426 (or resolves a registration-absent state explicitly); a test with no SW registration and a never-settling serviceWorker.ready proves the sweep completes, reports not-subscribed, and does not leak a pending promise per focus event; the 'sw-not-ready' copy distinguishes permanent unavailability (no registration / flag off) from initializing, or the UI derives the state from registration presence instead of a pending promise
- evidence: web/src/push/subscribe.test.ts new cases (deferred ready promise + fake timers); grep that no other unbounded serviceWorker.ready await remains in the sweep path; manual flag-enabled run without a SW shows the sweep settle in DevTools

- id: `rm-279` | track: reliability | priority: 96.0 | status: candidate (added 2026-09-30, run a923284c assess F1 + research C1a; verified no sibling lineage tracks Lint-job decomposition — the a666f8c0 lineage's rm-277 is the server.ts/aggregator.ts wiring-core split, a different surface)
- signals: the Main workflow's Lint job is cancelled at exactly its `timeout-minutes: 35` ceiling on EVERY push-to-main run since 2026-09-29 — run 36692888877 (head 31995a2, Lint 08:56:40Z→09:31:59Z = 35m19s), 36599485283 (278d350, 35m19s), 36576833307 (65f05db, 35m17s) — while the same runs' five sibling jobs go green and a PR-context Main run completes the full matrix green in ~12m (36738240926, 2026-09-30 15:38Z fire); the 20→35 bump (.github/workflows/main.yaml:33-36, rm-13640 rider; context doc docs/solutions/workflow-issues/ci-validator-poll-window-vs-cold-lint-2026-09-29.md) did not fix it, so the overrun is structural, not transient: cold repo-wide eslint over ROADMAP.md's mega single-line signals blobs — rm-103's signals line alone is ~10KB, with the fleet measuring everything-else linting in seconds while that line exceeds 400s on a loaded box, and the shared runner pool is slower still; consequences: no green Main run as a unit for the last 3 merges (last green 163ff11 2026-09-29T10:19Z), the repo's own GraphQL statusCheckRollup reads FAILURE (live probe 2026-09-30, run a923284c research), and filling rm-116's required checks before this lands would deadlock every merge behind a job that cannot finish
- acceptance: (a) the Lint job decomposed so no single step can hit the ceiling — a repo-wide eslint step that EXCLUDES ROADMAP.md with a bounded timeout, plus a dedicated ROADMAP.md step linting the file per-line (or per-block) through a stdin-fed eslint invocation with a per-line timeout so one pathological line fails visibly instead of hanging the job; AND (b) a companion ledger refactor splitting the rm-103-class mega signals lines into multi-line lists so the pathology cannot regrow (coordinated with rm-104's render hygiene so the generator emits the split shape); proof = a green push-to-main Lint run at the new shape plus one follow-up push run green (not luck)

- id: `rm-280` | track: reliability | priority: 84.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-30 (added 2026-09-30, run a923284c assess F2 + research C1b; cycle-1 batch B1 in-tree at 31995a2, pending landing — implement c583a49a, full-suite green on ephemeral validation PR #306 all 10 checks; post-merge live acceptance owed: one green workflow_dispatch + the 2026-10-05 05:23Z weekly cron; grows the rm-179/rm-225 canary family)

### CodeQL default-setup false positive on the PKCE S256 test digest (recurring landing-pipeline hazard)
- id: `rm-281` | track: security | priority: 62.0 | status: candidate (added 2026-09-30, run a923284c assess F3 + research C1c; assess premise corrected at mint — see signals)
- signals: proved live 2026-09-30 on sibling run 2ee1c4841e9d's validation PR (branch conductor/ci-cfdc7acc008f): the bare GHAS "CodeQL" check (default setup, "new alerts in changed code") failed with annotation `js/insufficient-password-hash` at test/auth-pkce.test.ts:125:41-48 — the RFC-7636-mandated `createHash('sha256').update(verifier)` binding proof over the cookie-derived verifier — while the same tree's Main run 36738240926 concluded SUCCESS 15:50:58Z, so CodeQL was the sole red check; CORRECTION at mint (this run's verification): test/auth-pkce.test.ts is NOT on main at 31995a2 — it rides 2ee1c4841e9d's pending candidate lineage, whose current snapshot (origin/conductor/ci-457f6a6fc1e6) has ALREADY refactored the proof (grep: no createHash; the site comment reads "needs no direct cookie-value hash here", binding delegated to /auth/callback's 403 behavior); what remains durably unowned: (a) verify the refactored content's CodeQL check is green at that lineage's landing (not re-probable at mint — api.github.com unreachable from this host), and (b) the recurrence class — ANY future test hashing a cookie/token-derived string re-triggers the false positive, and the cure menu was re-derived from scratch twice in one day on 2026-09-30; diagnostic worth pinning: check-run ANNOTATIONS stay queryable via `gh api repos/codeo1io/dashboard/check-runs/<id>/annotations` even after the validator deletes its refs
- acceptance: at the 2ee1c4841e9d landing (or before, on that lineage) the carrying PR's CodeQL check is green with the annotation addressed by suppression-or-refactor, recorded in the landing batch doc; the ledger carries the recipe so it is never re-derived a third time: test-only PKCE/token digests take `// codeql[js/insufficient-password-hash]` on the line immediately above the site (test-only edits keep validation_digest invariant when the digest derives from changed src surfaces) OR the no-direct-hash refactor, chosen once and cited thereafter
- evidence: annotation js/insufficient-password-hash at test/auth-pkce.test.ts:125:41-48 from run 2ee1c4841e9d's check-run (queryable post-ref-deletion via the check-runs annotations API); Main run 36738240926 conclusion SUCCESS 2026-09-30T15:50:58Z vs the red CodeQL check on the same head; `git show origin/conductor/ci-457f6a6fc1e6:test/auth-pkce.test.ts` (no createHash; refactor comment at :127); absence on main: `git log --all --oneline -- 'test/auth-pkce*'` → only the ephemeral validation snapshot 457f6a6

### operator-stream runtime: module-singleton stream handle vs StrictMode double-mount (dev-only today)
- id: `rm-283` | track: reliability | priority: 16.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-30 (added 2026-09-30, run a923284c assess F7; cycle-1 batch B2 in-tree at 31995a2, pending landing — implement c583a49a, 60/60 root + 50/50 web suite + check-types green, full-suite green on ephemeral validation PR #306; hazard is dev/fixture-only — production mounts a single instance)
- signals: web/src/operator/runtime.ts keeps `_activeStreamHandle` (:193) and `_activeStreamRunId` (:198) as module-level singletons shared across ALL runtime instances, so under React StrictMode's double-mount the first mount's cleanup path can late-resolve its loader and close the singleton handle AFTER mount2 attached its own stream — closing mount2's just-opened stream; no test covers a two-instance lifetime overlap
- acceptance: stream ownership scoped per instance (or the late-resolving cleanup made idempotent against a handle it no longer owns), plus a test that mounts two runtimes and asserts mount2's stream survives mount1's cleanup; the singleton comment updated to state the invariant
- evidence: web/src/operator/runtime.ts:193/:198 and the maybeCleanup→_closeActiveStream path; the new two-instance lifecycle test green
- rider (2026-09-30, run a923284c independent_review d941735c): instance-scoping closes the HANDLE half only — `close()` still clears the stale-eviction-protection marker through a GLOBAL-DOM lookup keyed on runId alone (`web/src/operator/runtime.ts:256-257`: `document.querySelector('[data-run-id=…]')` then `delete card.dataset.streamAttached`); the marker is what `public/operator-run-index.js` reads as active-stream state (:390/:460/:512; set by `markRunStreamAttached` :271-282, invoked from runtime.ts:362-363), so with two instances on the SAME runId (StrictMode remount preserves the expanded hash) instance1's late cleanup deletes the shared card's marker while instance2's stream is still live and the still-attached card now reads unattached — dev/fixture-only exposure today (production mounts a single instance); next-cycle candidate: owner-token guard before marker deletion (the marker records which owner set it; close deletes only its own) plus a cross-instance same-runId regression test asserting instance2's marker survives instance1's cleanup — tracked here (open scope), not rm-107 (separate surfaces)
- rider (2026-09-30, run a923284c implement c583a49a + full_tests 8dcf0b7c): implemented in this tree — stream ownership instance-scoped with idempotent close-what-you-own semantics, two new lifecycle tests added (60/60 root suite + 50/50 web suite green, `pnpm check-types` green); full-suite green via ephemeral validation PR #306 (10/10 checks); flip to completed at landing with the suite URLs

### Bound the /auth/logout body read for real (the rm-268 cap is post-hoc)
- id: `rm-497` | track: security | priority: 66.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-30 in-tree, validated pre-review (run f3fbd7d9 implement eb7a0ae6; validation run f3fbd7d9 targeted_tests 3f654118 + full_tests c7c68505: local gates green — check-types rc=0, repo-wide eslint rc=0 in 94s, full vitest 49 files/2309 tests rc=0 — then authoritative ephemeral-PR gates ALL 9 checks SUCCESS twice, PR #307 @ 6753f5ebba70 and PR #321 @ f64bbd0ecb8a, no visual check because the workflow is path-filtered and this batch touches no web/ source; pending landing; original: added 2026-09-30, run f3fbd7d9 assess f8b3f78a — escalates completed rm-268, whose cap is the defect; dated rider 2026-09-30, run f3fbd7d9 implement eb7a0ae6: IMPLEMENTED as one shared reader — NEW src/read-body.ts exports readBodyCapped + MAX_REQUEST_BODY_BYTES=16384 (constant renamed from listener's MAX_INGEST_BODY_BYTES; src/listener/contract.ts:54 keeps its own module-local MAX_BODY_BYTES=8192, a different bound at a different layer, and grep shows no stale references), routes/listener.ts drops its private copy and imports the shared module, and routes/auth.ts logout replaces rm-268's post-hoc `c.req.text()` + UTF-16 `.length` check with the incremental reader that counts wire BYTES READ; all three acceptance shapes pinned by NEW tests in test/auth.test.ts — endless-chunked (cancelled at the 3rd 8KiB chunk without draining the socket), finite-no-content-length, lying-content-length, each 413ing at the cap — while rm-268's honest-header test stays green ; renumbered at the 2026-10-03 integrate, conflict case 8a29b5ff990347cdbf947fb13fbe481b — minted as rm-280 at base 31995a2 and renumbered above the all-lineage ceiling rm-496 because main's rm-280 (run a923284c — GraphQL canary install step) owns that id with another meaning ; LANDED at this integrate 2026-10-03 (conflict case 8a29b5ff990347cdbf947fb13fbe481b): src/read-body.ts + the listener/auth call-site rewrite + the three 413-shape tests merged onto main's tree clean (no textual conflict — this entry auto-merged ahead of the conflicted rm-276 hunk))
- signals: src/routes/auth.ts:199 pins MAX_LOGOUT_BODY_BYTES to 16384 but enforces it post-hoc — the precheck at auth.ts:202-205 only sees an honest content-length, `await c.req.text()` at auth.ts:207 buffers the ENTIRE stream before the length check at auth.ts:208 fires, so a chunked or lying-header request reads unbounded bytes into memory on a pre-auth public route (isPublicPath admits /auth/logout, src/server.ts:726); secondary defect in the same lines: `rawBody.length` counts UTF-16 code units, not bytes, so even honest bodies are capped approximately; the bounded reader already exists in-tree but is module-private — readBodyCapped (src/routes/listener.ts:103, incremental, same 16384 budget via MAX_INGEST_BODY_BYTES at listener.ts:21) — and rm-268's landed test only pins the honest-header shape (test/auth.test.ts:466-477 sends a 17_000-byte body with a truthful content-length), which is how the hole survived its review
- acceptance:
  - the logout read goes through the shared incremental reader: hoist readBodyCapped out of routes/listener.ts into a shared module and consume it for BOTH ingest and logout — one constant, one code path, no second copy of the bound
  - a body that exceeds the cap mid-stream is cut off at the boundary and 413s without awaiting the rest (chunked transfer-encoding, no-content-length, and lying-header shapes each pinned by a test)
  - the cap counts BYTES READ, not post-hoc UTF-16 code units
- evidence: pnpm test green with the three new /auth/logout 413 shapes; grep shows `c.req.text()` gone from routes/auth.ts; both call sites import the same hoisted reader; test names cite the three shapes

### Both SSE parsers keep only the last `data:` line of a record (spec: concatenate with \n)
- id: `rm-484` | track: reliability | priority: 36.0 | status: candidate (added 2026-09-30, run 23d39aa7 assess F2 + research R3; the deviation is byte-identical in both parsers, so the fix rides rm-252's both-parsers acceptance discipline and coordinates with rm-253's wire-or-fold decision for the dead server twin; renumbered at the 2026-10-03 integrate, conflict case 9abfab343ad54dd9a1a5aa286954a308 — minted as rm-279 at base 31995a2 and renumbered above the all-lineage ceiling rm-483 because main's rm-279 (run a923284c — Lint-job decomposition) owns that id with another meaning)
- signals: src/gateway/operator-sse-reader.ts:124-125 reassigns `dataLine = line.slice('data:'.length).trim()` per data line (last-wins) and public/operator-stream.js:216-217 is byte-identical in the live browser twin; WHATWG HTML 'Interpreting an event stream' (§9.2.6) requires consecutive data lines to be joined with U+000A into ONE event payload — a spec-compliant multi-line payload (e.g. pretty-printed JSON) would keep only its last line, fail JSON.parse, and drop silently as a typed parse failure with no signal; latent today (the gateway emits single-line JSON frames; .slim/clonedeps is absent from run worktrees so emission cannot be re-verified here — grounded on the fork's own parsers plus the spec), but rm-252's absorb explicitly grows the payload surface (1.7.0/1.8.0 run-status fields, checkout provenance), which is exactly when a pretty-printed or wrapped frame becomes plausible
- acceptance: both parsers concatenate consecutive data lines with \n in ONE change (src/gateway/operator-sse-reader.ts + public/operator-stream.js, per the both-parsers discipline); test/operator-sse-reader.test.ts and test/operator-stream-core.test.ts each gain a record whose JSON payload is split across two data lines and assert the reassembled frame parses; test/sse-parser.property.test.ts extends its generator to emit multi-line data records; existing single-line fixtures stay green unchanged
- evidence: parser diffs + the three suite extensions; grep that no `data:` reassignment (last-wins) remains in either parser; pnpm test green

- signals: .github/workflows/main.yaml:32-35 comment says the rider provides "20 min self-kills" and "the Test job's 40 is the precedent", but :36 sets the Lint job timeout-minutes: 35 and every other main.yaml job is 20 (:58 design-check, :86 check-types, :106 test, :128 check-workflows, :153 test-scripts-load) — no job is 40, and the Test job the comment cites is itself 20; the 35 ceiling is load-bearing (cold repo-wide eslint must self-kill before the CI validator's ~36-minute poll window — and branch-level rider bumps never ride validation, which always builds on origin/main's workflows), so a maintainer trusting the comment could raise or lower the wrong number in either direction

### SSE readers: CRLF split across read chunks forges a phantom record boundary
- id: `rm-477` | track: reliability | priority: 62.0 | status: completed 2026-09-29 (run dbe8fb1696ea implement 8951609c; landed at the 2026-10-03 integrate, conflict case 115dab70b53d48e9b06b4c5f00d97497 (integration b3b3bdfe5fb3, ours d2d8793 / theirs c32e6b7) — minted as rm-477 at base 38afd906 and renumbered above the all-lineage ceiling rm-476 (verified across every local ref's ROADMAP, fleet terminal-estate refs included) because main's rm-477 is a superseded tombstone (ex rm-255 of run 084619327305, folded into rm-252); this entry keeps the candidate lineage and body below: pending-CR hold landed in BOTH readers — server inline at the read-loop seam, twin via exported `appendStreamChunk(buffer, decoded)` used by the loop and declared in public/operator-stream.d.ts; deterministic split-at-CR + split-between-separators regressions in the reader suite went red pre-fix (`[ready]` vs `[ready, status]`) and green post-fix, chunking-invariance properties at 300 runs in both suites; reader 133/133 + twin 369/369 + property suite green; tsc ×2 RC=0; targeted eslint RC=0; validated pre-review 2026-09-29: impacted-tests 825/825 across a 19-suite set + full ephemeral CI 10/10 green on PR #261, snapshot 7ddda611bead; prevention rule documented in docs/solutions/logic-errors/sse-crlf-split-across-read-chunks-2026-09-29.md) (added 2026-09-29, run dbe8fb1696ea assess F1/F2)
- signals: both SSE readers normalize CRLF per chunk with no pending-CR state — src/gateway/operator-sse-reader.ts:532 (createOperatorSseReader's feed) and the production twin public/operator-stream.js:2489 — so a read() chunk ending `\r` followed by a chunk starting `\n` normalizes to `\n` + `\n`: two truncated records that both fail parseSseFrame and are dropped with NO onError dispatched, i.e. the frame vanishes silently (the production reader is the operator view's own parser, served flag-independent at /static/ and loaded by web/src/views/Operator.tsx). PROVED LIVE 2026-09-29 (attempt d060ece7 scratch /tmp/d060ece7-sse-cr-split.mjs driving createOperatorSseReader over three feeds): single-chunk control → frames `[ready, status]`; split mid-payload control → `[ready, status]`; split exactly at the CR → `[ready]` with errors=none. CRLF is an asserted-supported input (whole-record CRLF fixtures at test/operator-sse-reader.test.ts:1087/:1106) and the suite header at :12 documents 'Partial-chunk reassembly: frame split across two chunks is parsed once whole' — reassembly holds for payload splits but not line-ending splits; rm-114's landed line-ending-invariance properties generate whole-record chunkings, never chunk-boundary splits
- acceptance: both parsers carry pending-CR state across chunks (or defer normalization until after boundary search) — the fix lands in BOTH src/gateway/operator-sse-reader.ts and public/operator-stream.js exactly once, riding rm-253's wire-or-fold decision (if the twin is folded, the browser parser is the only one to fix); regression tests in both suites splitting a CRLF frame exactly at the CR and exactly at the LF; the rm-114 property suite gains a chunk-boundary-split generator case; LF-only streams keep byte-identical behavior (no new record emission)
- evidence: the live repro (controls vs CR-split: `[ready, status]` vs `[ready]`, errors=none) ported into test/operator-sse-reader.test.ts as a named case; a twin fixture in the public/operator-stream.js suite; both suites green

### /static operator assets ship with no client-caching policy
- id: `rm-478` | track: performance | priority: 44.0 | status: completed 2026-09-29 (run dbe8fb1696ea implement 8951609c; landed at the 2026-10-03 integrate, conflict case 115dab70b53d48e9b06b4c5f00d97497 (integration b3b3bdfe5fb3, ours d2d8793 / theirs c32e6b7) — minted as rm-478 at base 38afd906 and renumbered above the all-lineage ceiling rm-476 (verified across every local ref's ROADMAP, fleet terminal-estate refs included) because main's rm-478 is a superseded tombstone (ex rm-256 of run 084619327305, folded into rm-254); this entry keeps the candidate lineage and body below: `operatorRuntimeCaching` middleware (sha256 ETag over the served file, `Cache-Control: no-cache`, 304 on If-None-Match) wired to all three /static runtime-JS mounts in src/server.ts; hashed-immutable declined — the runtime twins are unversioned paths, a filename change is a loader/rollout change, not a header change; test/static-assets.test.ts 88/88 with 5 new assertions covering policy + conditional-request 304; validated pre-review 2026-09-29: 88/88 re-confirmed in the 19-suite impacted set + full ephemeral CI 10/10 green on PR #261, snapshot 7ddda611bead) (added 2026-09-29, run dbe8fb1696ea assess F4 + research C1)
- signals: the three unversioned operator JS files (public/operator-stream.js and the run-index/launch twins, ~4,300 lines / ~150KB) are served unconditionally via serveStatic at src/server.ts:955-975 with NO Cache-Control or ETag, while the service worker is a self-purging kill-switch (web/src/sw.ts unregisters and deletes its caches on activate) — so there is NO caching layer at all: every operator view load (web/src/views/Operator.tsx is the consumer) refetches the full ~150KB on heuristic caching alone; test/static-assets.test.ts carries 19 CSP assertions but zero caching assertions, and nothing in this ledger matches cache/etag/immutable on /static (grep 2026-09-29); the hashed /assets/* build output is a separate, already-hash-addressable surface and deliberately out of scope here
- acceptance: an explicit recorded policy for /static/* — either no-cache + ETag revalidation middleware or content-hash filenames + immutable (rm-172's SPA-shell handler is the in-repo precedent for an explicit header policy; hashed filenames require updating the Operator.tsx loader to the hashed names); test/static-assets.test.ts gains Cache-Control/ETag assertions pinning the chosen policy so the choice cannot silently regress
- evidence: curl -I on /static/operator-stream.js shows the chosen header in the ephemeral-PR validation; static-assets suite green

### Aggregator cold-start in-flight degradation contract
- id: `rm-197` | track: reliability | priority: 59.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-25 in-tree, validated pre-review (cycle-14 batch B1, run ec07634c implement c3bbc0c8; minted above rm-196 after the 8bbff5c6 renumber took the original rm-191 allocation; validation run ec07634c targeted_tests 8b11a67e + full_tests 1cab3836: local focused 248/248, then authoritative ephemeral-PR gates ALL 9 checks SUCCESS twice — PR #146 @ f2a23bd3 and PR #147 @ e55dcb1 — pending landing; review fix 07afb0e5 closed independent-review F1: the two server.ts transports the unit-test fakes hid are now wired — see evidence)
- signals: between start() and the first refresh completing, getSnapshot() returned {repos: [], staleBanner: false} — authoritative-looking fresh-empty (aggregator.ts:929 at 6fd7d04); start() gated interval installation behind await refresh() with no re-entry guard; zero timeout/AbortSignal wiring across the Octokit constructions (app-client.ts, installations.ts), so a stalled serial first walk yields minutes of false-fresh empty; the FAILED first-refresh path DID banner (markSnapshotStale, :665) — only the in-flight window was uncovered. 6fd7d04 (PR #140) closed the warm half of rm-112, making this the last false-fresh gap
- acceptance: (1) cold getSnapshot serves bannered empty (explicit not-fresh) while any refresh may be in flight; (2) every GitHub transport call time-bounded (request.timeout via the AppClientOptions seam, default 30s); (3) interval installed independently of the first refresh completing; (4) stalled transport trips the existing FAILED-refresh banner path
- evidence: implemented: cold branch returns staleBanner:true (aggregator.ts getSnapshot), AppClientOptions.requestTimeoutMs + exported GITHUB_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS=30_000 wired into the ThrottledOctokit ctor (app-client.ts) and the installation-token Octokit (installations.ts) — metadata.ts constructs no Octokit (rides the app client); start() installs the interval then awaits the bounded first walk; both EMPTY_SNAPSHOT literals (server.ts, routes/api.ts) aligned bannered; no-provider defaults follow. Tests: cold-window banner, interval-installed-before-first-refresh-resolves (deferred enumerate), double-start no-op, double-stop single-clear (test/aggregator.test.ts); focused suites 248/248 green at 6fd7d04. Review fix (independent review 6f4e6190 F1, P1): acceptance (2) was unmet at implement time — the two transports constructed in server.ts escaped the batch's three-module fence and the unit fakes hid them; now ALL FIVE transport constructions carry the 30s bound (app-client ThrottledOctokit, installations Octokit, server.ts metadata `new Octokit` + server.ts per-repo `graphql.defaults`, auth/oauth.ts via AbortSignal.timeout); source-contract gate test/transport-timeout-contract.test.ts pins the wiring (census 4 matching construction sites, floor ≥4); the duplicated EMPTY_SNAPSHOT literals were consolidated to the exported COLD_START_SNAPSHOT (review F3)

### Boot-time last-known snapshot bridge
- id: `rm-198` | track: reliability | priority: 34.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-26 (run 9cb75bf5 cycle-15 B2, minted in that batch as rm-200 and renumbered onto this landed id — landed meanings own ids; the batch authored at base 6fd7d04 minted above a then-max rm-196 that main's cycle-14 consumed with a different rm-200; decision gate satisfied: rm-197 landed. Implemented as an injectable SnapshotStore seam with a JSON-file-backed store — src/github/snapshot-store.ts (DASHBOARD_SNAPSHOT_CACHE, fail-open on any load/persist fault, 2 MiB size cap, atomic tmp+rename writes) rather than the acceptance's node:sqlite sketch; restart-only semantics preserved (no intra-cycle TTL, superseding the rm-112 rationale) and the seam is injectable so a node:sqlite-backed store swaps in without touching the aggregator; the boot seed is served forced-stale under rm-197's banner contract, and ALL 7 snapshot writes route through one setSnapshot site (single persist chokepoint). The aaa84dff-credited fail-closed rehydrate-after-metadata-read answer is implemented as scrubLastGoodAgainstDenylist: a fresh metadata read re-scrubs the boot-seeded/carried last-good snapshot BEFORE any serve path, so a repo redacted between persist and restart is never re-served (the 2026-06-25 redaction concern, closed downstream of the same metadata gate the live cycle obeys); 13 new tests (9 aggregator bridge + 4 store). Originally: candidate, added 2026-09-25, cycle-14 batch, run ec07634c; decision-gated — supersedes the rm-112 TTL-removal rationale restart-only, never intra-cycle; gated on rm-197 landing first; PARALLEL RE-PROPOSE credited 2026-09-25 at the aaa84dff integrate: the cycle-13 batch's rm-188 (dedupe, item released) reached the same node:sqlite restart-bridge frame independently and adds the fail-closed answer to the 2026-06-25 redaction concern — rehydrate only after a successful metadata/repos.yaml read with the denylist reapplied before serving, i.e. downstream of the same metadata gate the live cycle obeys)
- signals: cold start serves bannered empty (rm-197) until the first walk completes even when a prior process held a good snapshot minutes earlier; the rm-112 TTL-removal rationale recorded freshness invariance, not a prohibition on a restart-only bridge
- acceptance: persist the last good snapshot (node:sqlite mirroring the listener-store pattern) and serve it at boot bannered-stale until the first refresh completes; decision frame: restart-only semantics with no intra-cycle TTL, no API-budget motive, explicit supersession of the rm-112 rationale recorded in the implementing batch doc
- evidence: gated on rm-197's in-flight contract landing first; a future cycle decides the bridge against the decision frame recorded in docs/prioritization/2026-09-25-cycle-14-batch.md §Parked

### Redaction single-char token boundary
- id: `rm-200` | track: security | priority: 41.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-25 in-tree, validated pre-review (cycle-14 batch B2, run ec07634c implement c3bbc0c8; validation run ec07634c targeted_tests 8b11a67e + full_tests 1cab3836: local focused 248/248 incl. the boundary suites, authoritative ephemeral-PR gates ALL 9 checks SUCCESS twice — PR #146 @ f2a23bd3 and PR #147 @ e55dcb1 — pending landing; review fix 07afb0e5 closed independent-review F2: the full pair token is now replaced unanchored — see evidence)
- signals: redactRepoIdentityFromText filtered its token list with token.length > 1, dropping GitHub-legal 1-char owner/repo tokens from log scrubbing entirely; the property suite's nameArb enforced min length 2, so the class was structurally untestable
- acceptance: boundary-anchored replacement covers tokens of length 1+ without mangling adjacent prose; nameArb widened to min length 1; regression test with 1-char owner and name proving standalone tokens scrub while embedded occurrences survive
- evidence: implemented: escapeRegExp + boundary-anchored (no word, dot or dash character adjacent) longest-first replacement in aggregator.ts; nameArb widened {1,30}→{0,30}; the identity property's assertion refined to boundary-anchored non-survival (not.toContain was too strong for tokens lexically embedded in scaffold words, e.g. 'one' inside 'alone' — embedding is not identity survival); explicit unit suite: standalone 1-char + full pair redacted, unrelated words ('cat','bravo') intact, dotted/dashed names ('a.txt','b-c') untouched; focused suites 248/248 green. Review fix (independent review 6f4e6190 F2, P3): the anchoring let a complete `owner/name` pair survive adjacent to '-'/'.' ### Operator stream stall watchdog and a Last-Event-ID resume decision
- id: `rm-220` | track: reliability | priority: 50.0 | status: candidate (added 2026-09-24, run 449ed1a5 assess F5 + research; complements rm-114's parser work; RENUMBERED rm-164→rm-220 at the 2026-09-25 integrate of run 449ed1a5, conflict case e7b03a2f — main's rm-164 is the landed prose-residue guard)
- signals: the operator SSE client times out only the first frame (public/operator-stream.js) — after connect, an open-but-silent connection never trips a client-side watchdog, and there is no Last-Event-ID support anywhere (0 hits across public/operator-stream.js, src/gateway/operator-sse-reader.ts, src/routes/operator.ts), so frames emitted during a reconnect gap are silently lost; WHATWG's server-sent-events processing model defines both lastEventId and reconnection handling (html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/server-sent-events.html, verified 2026-09-24), and a wedged gateway behind an open proxy freezes the operator UI indefinitely with no signal
- acceptance: an idle-frame watchdog (missed-heartbeat or wall-clock) marks the stream stale visibly in the UI; an explicit decision recorded on resume — adopt Last-Event-ID end-to-end (gateway reader support required) or decline with a written rationale and a reconnect-gap bound; tests cover stall detection and the chosen reconnect behavior
- evidence: a test injecting a silent frame stream trips the watchdog; the decision + rationale in docs; the UI shows a stale state rather than a frozen live view

### Cache gateway-mode session validation (bounded revocation-latency decision)
- id: `rm-419` | track: reliability | priority: 52.0 | status: implemented 2026-09-25 in-tree, renumbered from rm-207 at the 2026-09-25 ci-gate merge (parallel lineage landed its own rm-207), renumbered AGAIN rm-222 -> rm-419 at the 2026-10-03 integrate (conflict case c709c4d4f1ec4b8a816b755f38126402) because main's landed ledger owns rm-222 (deadline-racing/stall-watchdog, landed 828a1e6); validated pre-review (run bf5d7753 cycle:1 batch B1, implement 94a9bc78: NEW src/gateway/session-cache.ts — POSITIVE-ONLY 15s-TTL session-validation cache (upstream errors and rejected sessions are never cached, so fail-closed stays per-request and a transient upstream failure can never wedge a permanent deny or grant — the acceptance's negative-cache alternative was deliberately not taken; bound decision recorded at the implementation site), monotonic injected clock, dual-bound freshness (age < TTL AND the session's own expiresAt still future) with lazy stale-entry delete on read, 256-key cap with full-map reset on overflow — wired into src/server.ts gateway auth branch, 7 tests in test/gateway-auth-session-cache.test.ts; validation targeted_tests 08d1ad29 + full_tests 224f12e2: local focused 127 server + 39 web green, authoritative ephemeral-PR ALL 10 checks SUCCESS ×3 — #185 @ a0c4ed53, #189 @ 8e7d7e2b, #191 @ 3ec1d3aa on base 021c1001 — pending review/landing. ID COLLISION: a parallel cycle-17 campaign landed unrelated visual-paths work also labeled rm-207 — renumber at integrate per the ledger convention; at the 2026-10-03 integrate the candidate landed as part of the c709c4d4 merge — see the header INTEGRATE-MERGE 2026-10-03 note)
- signals: src/server.ts:704-727 at origin/main 20ade64 (wired by the cycle-14 rm-197 review fix) — the gateway auth branch builds `createOperatorServerFetch` and calls `client.getCurrentSession()` on EVERY protected request: all /api/*, the operator proxy, and every SSE connection setup pay a synchronous upstream roundtrip (10s timeout, fail-closed), while the operator UI polls at 30s cadence and SSE reconnects re-trigger it; a gateway brownout couples every dashboard request to upstream health even with a locally-valid session cookie, doubling tail latency under normal load
- acceptance:
  - session-validation results are cached with an explicit, documented revocation bound (short TTL ≤15s positive/negative cache, or a cache keyed by the session cookie value invalidated on cookie rotation) — the chosen bound is recorded next to the implementation as a decision (mirrors the rm-129 rate-limiter decision-record pattern)
  - cache MISS path and TTL expiry both fail closed exactly like today (upstream error → unauthenticated); a cache HIT must not outlive the cookie it validated
  - explicit tests: cached-hit behavior, TTL expiry re-validation, upstream-error-during-refresh fails closed, and revocation within one TTL
- evidence: src/server.ts middleware diff; a new suite in `pnpm test` covering the four cases above; a comment at the cache site documenting the bound and its security tradeoff

### Hashed `/assets/*` bundles ship with zero caching policy — immutable serving plus pinned policies for unversioned statics
- id: `rm-555` | track: performance | priority: 40.0 | status: implemented 2026-10-03 (run 7a4d9070f340 cycle-1 batch B1, implement dea9b5b0: src/server.ts hashedAssetImmutablePolicy + unversionedCachePolicy, both-direction pins in test/static-assets.test.ts; recorded decision: uniform `no-cache` for unversioned surfaces — serveStatic adds no validators there, so it degenerates to refetch-every-time, matching today's wire behavior but explicit and immune to RFC 9111 §4.2.2 heuristic caching; `/sw.js` + `/registerSW.js` keep their pre-existing stricter no-cache,no-store,must-revalidate) | originally candidate added 2026-10-03, run 7a4d9070f340 assess 2c73c475 F1 + research 1ab22f82 R1; verified unowned — rm-478's completed entry declined hashed-immutable only for its unversioned `/static/*` twins and left `/assets/*` explicitly out of scope, and no other item owns this surface
- signals: src/server.ts:1169 serves `/assets/*` with no Cache-Control and no ETag (live-probed 2026-10-03 via `await buildDashboardApp({...}).request(...)` → 200, cache-control null, etag null on the ~285 KB JS bundle and its CSS twin), so returning operators re-download the bundle on heuristic caching; every `/assets/*` filename is content-hashed BY CONFIG (web/vite.config.ts:75-79 pins entry/chunk/assetFileNames to `assets/[name]-[hash]`), making `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` representation-safe (RFC 8246); deploy-rollover is immediate because the SPA shell is no-store (src/server.ts:1071); @hono/node-server 2.1.3 serveStatic has no cacheControl option (ServeStaticOptions), so the fix is a wrapper middleware (rm-478's operatorRuntimeCaching precedent) or its onFound hook; the UNVERSIONED `/icon-*`, `/manifest.webmanifest`, `/privacy`, `/registerSW.js` and the src/server.ts:1081 shell variant are policy-null too and must NOT be immutable — the item pins explicit short-revalidation policies for them
- acceptance: `/assets/*` responses carry `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` with no ETag added (hashed names make validators redundant); `/icon-*`, `/manifest.webmanifest`, `/privacy`, `/registerSW.js` and the shell variants carry an explicit no-cache or short-max-age policy (recorded decision which) — never immutable; test/static-assets.test.ts pins BOTH directions (immutable present on a hashed asset, absent on an unversioned surface); if a compression item lands alongside, compressed `/assets/*` responses keep `Vary: Accept-Encoding` beside the immutable directive
- evidence: before/after live header probes (`buildDashboardApp` request and curl) recorded in the cycle batch doc; the vite hash config as the immutability basis; suite green; markdown lint clean; done 2026-10-03 — probe-rm555.mjs before/after in the delegate spool, 9 impacted suites 433/433 green, check-types 0, eslint 0
